## Analog Bag 'Contents Map' Tote (POD)

**Trend:** Analog bag & screen-free gifting (Test now) · **Format:** tote (print on demand) · **Asymmetry:** Roughly even (53/100)

**Buyer:** Analog-curious Gen Z/millennials, and people buying a tote to build an analog bag as a gift

### Super prompt

```text
You are an illustrator and print-on-demand designer. Your goal: produce the complete design package for an illustrated "contents map" tote bag, a canvas tote whose artwork shows what goes inside an analog bag, sold on Etsy through Printify.

BUYER AND JOB: Analog-curious Gen Z and millennials, and people buying a tote to build an analog bag as a gift. The bag is the object at the center of the trend, so this tote is both the gift and the container; it pairs with a printable "Analog Bag Refill Pack".

DELIVERABLE:
1. Phrase options: 12 original short phrases (2-4 words) for the top of the design in the spirit of "Gone analog", plain and warm, no puns on brands. Rank them and flag each "needs a USPTO trademark search before use"; never present any phrase as cleared.
2. Composition spec for a single-color line drawing on a 14x14 in square master (4200x4200 px at 300 DPI, transparent background), centered: a dashed outline of an open tote containing 6 items drawn in a loose, overlapping arrangement: a paperback with a blank cover, a boxy generic film camera (rangefinder-like rectangle, no instant-camera shape, no logo, no lens text), a ball of yarn with a crochet hook, a small folded pocket puzzle booklet, a pencil, and a small notebook. Each item gets a small number in a circle; a legend below reads like a map key (for example "1 a paperback 2 a camera 3 yarn & hook 4 a pocket puzzle 5 a pencil 6 a notebook"; write the final legend text). Give the item positions as a 3x3 grid layout and the phrase position.
3. Print rules: minimum line weight about 2 pt at final size; no fine hatching or tiny text under 14 pt; one ink color; text set later in a typed font, not drawn by the image AI. Ink versions: Graphite #2E2E2E on a natural tote, Denim #3E5C76 as an alternate, and Oat #F4EDE1 for a black tote.
4. Three image-generation prompts I can paste into an image model: (a) the main line drawing, (b) a looser hand-drawn alternate, (c) the six items as separate sticker-style icons for spin-offs. Each states the style, composition, colors with hex codes, aspect ratio 1:1, transparent or pure white background, and negatives (no text, no logos, no brand shapes, no people, no watermarks).
5. Typography: a font for the phrase (such as Playfair Display Italic or DM Serif Display) and for the legend (Caveat or Courier Prime), with sizes in points at the 14 in width.
6. A mockup shot list of 7 listing images (flat lay, packed with refill booklets, on a shoulder with no face, detail of the legend, size guide, gift-set image, color options) and a 3-line gift-set note that tells buyers how the refill pack pairs with the tote.

CONTENT RULES: Everything original; plain, friendly wording; reading level grade 6-8; no claims about health, focus or screen time.

DIFFERENTIATION: Illustration-led rather than slogan-only; the legend makes it a "map" of an analog bag; it coordinates with the Refill Pack's "Oat & Ink" palette (Oat #F4EDE1, Graphite #2E2E2E, Tomato #D9583B, Moss #6B7B4B, Denim #3E5C76, Butter #F2C94C).

CONSTRAINTS: No brands or trade dress: nothing resembling Polaroid, Instax or Fujifilm cameras, Brick or Light Phone devices, The Offline Club, or any newspaper puzzle or daily word-game branding; no book titles; no creators' names or likenesses; no song lyrics or film references.

OUTPUT FORMAT: Numbered sections 1-6 as above, written so I can work through them in order in Canva and Printify. End with a self-check: phrases flagged for a trademark search; the camera described as generic; line-weight and text-size rules stated; all three image prompts include negatives and a 1:1 aspect ratio; no brand names appear except in the "do not resemble" list. Fix anything that fails, then answer.
```

**Image prompt 1 (Main tote artwork: single-color contents-map line drawing (text added later in Canva); ChatGPT images (ask for a transparent PNG) or Ideogram):**

```text
Single-color line illustration in graphite #2E2E2E on a transparent background (or pure white #FFFFFF if transparency is not available): a dashed outline of an open tote bag seen from the front, holding six items in a loose overlapping arrangement: a paperback with a blank cover, a boxy generic rectangular film camera with a round lens and no logo or lettering, a ball of yarn with a crochet hook, a small folded booklet with a tiny blank grid, a pencil, a small spiral notebook. Each item has a small empty circle beside it for a number. Clean confident lines of even, thick weight (no fine hatching), friendly hand-drawn feel, centered with empty space above and below for text. No text, no letters, no numbers, no logos, no instant-camera shapes, no phones, no people, no watermarks. Aspect ratio 1:1.
```

```

Flat colors tomato #D9583B, moss #6B7B4B, denim #3E5C76, butter #F2C94C with graphite #2E2E2E outlines. No text, no letters, no logos, no instant-camera shapes, no people, no watermarks. No text, no logos, no phones, no people, no watermarks. Photorealistic, aspect ratio 4:3. --ar 4:3 --style raw --no text, logos, phone, people, watermark
```

### Build and export

1. Run the super prompt in Claude or ChatGPT; pick 2-3 phrases and check each in the USPTO trademark search (tmsearch.uspto.gov) and on Etsy for existing use before choosing one (not done in the input). If anything looks close, use another phrase.
2. Generate the main artwork; reject any result whose camera looks like a real brand or model. If you only get a white background, remove it (Canva Pro background remover or a free remover) and upscale to at least 4200 x 4200 px with an upscaler; for the crispest lines, trace it to vector in Inkscape (free) before export.
3. In Canva create a 4200 x 4200 px design, place the art, add the numbers in the circles, the legend (Caveat or Courier Prime) and the chosen phrase (Playfair Display Italic or DM Serif Display), keep all text above about 14 pt at print size, then download as PNG with a transparent background. Make an Oat #F4EDE1 ink version for a black tote.
4. In Printify choose a natural cotton canvas tote with a US print provider (compare base cost, ratings and production time in the catalog), download or view its print-area template, and scale the PNG to fit inside the print area with the composition centered.
5. Generate Printify mockups (front, close-up, both colors), connect Printify to your Etsy shop, set the price ($28 with free shipping built in), and publish. Add the print provider as a production partner in Etsy as Etsy requires for print-on-demand items.
6. Order one sample to your own address on Day 0; when it arrives, check line sharpness and placement, then photograph it packed with printed refill booklets and replace the mockups as the first listing photos.
7. Upload the same art to Redbubble (tote bags and stickers) as a no-cost secondary listing.

### Listing copy prompt

```text
You are an Etsy SEO copywriter. Write the Etsy listing for a print-on-demand canvas tote bag with an original illustrated "contents map" of an analog bag: line drawings of a paperback, a generic film camera, yarn and a crochet hook, a pocket puzzle booklet, a pencil and a notebook, each numbered with a map-style legend, and the phrase [YOUR CHOSEN PHRASE].
Product facts to fill from my Printify product page: tote model and size [SIZE], fabric [FABRIC], handle length [HANDLES], print method [PRINT METHOD], production time [DAYS], US shipping time [DAYS]; printed and shipped by my print partner [PROVIDER NAME]. Gift-set option: buyers can add the printable Analog Bag Refill Pack (separate digital listing) [LINK].

Produce:
1. TITLE under 140 characters starting with the buyer's search phrase (choose from "Analog Bag Tote", "Book Lover Tote Bag", "Canvas Tote Bag Gift"), then the design (illustrated contents map, paperback, camera, yarn) and gift use. Sentence case; show the character count.
2. 13 TAGS of 20 characters or fewer (show each count), no repeats, from phrases like: analog bag, analog bag tote, canvas tote bag, book lover tote, reader gift, crochet tote, gift for her, screen free gift, cute tote bag, illustrated tote, bookish gift, aesthetic tote, christmas gift tote.
3. DESCRIPTION: a 2-line opening; what the illustration shows; tote specifications from the facts above; care instructions (cold wash inside out or spot clean; check the provider's care guidance); production and shipping times with a clear "order by [DATE] for Christmas delivery" line that I will set from my provider's cutoffs; the gift-set note; a line that the design was created with AI assistance and finished by me, and that it is printed by a production partner; a 4-question FAQ (What size is it? Is the print one color? Can I get it on a black tote? When will it arrive?).
RULES: No trademarked or brand terms anywhere (no camera, phone, puzzle, newspaper or app brands, no named clubs or creators); do not call the camera by any brand or model. No claims about health, focus or screen time. Do not promise delivery dates the provider does not guarantee. Output in order: Title, Tags, Description.
```

### Where to list

- **Etsy (via Printify)** (primary): Gift buyers shop Etsy for illustrated totes; the Printify integration handles printing, shipping and order sync with no inventory.
- **Redbubble** (secondary): Zero-cost second listing for the same art on totes and stickers; low margin but no extra work after upload.
- **Instagram** (traffic): Photos of the real tote packed as an analog bag fit the trend's visual style.
- **Pinterest** (traffic): Gift-set pins (tote plus refill booklets) catch 'analog bag ideas' and gift searches through early December.

**Positioning:** The bag at the center of the trend, drawn as a map of what goes inside it: an illustration-led canvas tote that is both the gift and the container, sold as a gift set with the printable refill pack.

### Launch plan

- **Day 0 (Thu Oct 15, 2026):** Publish the Etsy listing from Printify mockups at $28 with free shipping (input deadline: by Oct 15) and order one sample to your address.
- **Day 1 (Fri Oct 16):** Upload the art to Redbubble (tote and stickers).
- **Day 2 (Sat Oct 17):** Cross-link: add the tote to the Refill Pack's listing photos and description as a gift-set idea, and link the Refill Pack in the tote description.
- **Day 4 (Mon Oct 19):** Post 3 Pinterest pins (tote mockup, tote plus booklets gift set, legend close-up).
- **Day 9 (Sat Oct 24, estimate):** Sample arrives (production plus shipping time varies; estimate). Check print quality, photograph it packed as an analog bag, and replace the first two listing photos.
- **Day 12 (Tue Oct 27):** Post the real tote on Instagram and in a 'pack my analog bag' TikTok/Reel alongside the refill booklets.
- **Day 21 (Thu Nov 5):** Review views and favorites; adjust the title and first photo, and add an 'order by [provider cutoff] for Christmas' line.
- **Day 36 (Fri Nov 20):** Continue check: any tote sales by Nov 20 means keep it and push gift-set pins. Pivot if views come but no sales: offer the illustration as a sticker sheet or printable art print instead.
- **Day 51 (Sat Dec 5):** Stop check: no sales by Dec 5 (the practical print-on-demand cutoff for Christmas delivery) means stop promoting it; leave it listed passively or deactivate it.

### Marketing channels

| Channel | What to do | How often |
|---|---|---|
| Pinterest | Gift-set pins of the tote packed with the refill booklets, linking to the tote listing. | 3 pins in week 1, then 2 per week until Dec 5 |
| Instagram | Real-sample photos and a packing Reel once the sample arrives. | 2 posts per week from Day 12 to Dec 5 |
| TikTok | Include the tote in the Refill Pack's 'pack my analog bag' videos rather than separate promotion. | In 1 of every 3 Refill Pack videos |
| Redbubble | Upload once with clear tags; no active promotion. | Once |
| Etsy SEO | Organic only, no ads; refresh tags at Day 21. The keywords below are untested suggestions, not measured volumes. | Once at Day 21 |

**Search phrases to test (suggestions, not measured volumes):** analog bag tote, analog bag, book lover tote bag, canvas tote bag gift, crochet tote bag, reader gift, illustrated tote bag, screen free gift, aesthetic tote bag, bookish gift

**Hooks:**
- A tote that maps what goes inside an analog bag.
- Building an analog bag as a gift? Start with the bag.
- Paperback, yarn, pencil, puzzle: the map of my analog bag.
- Gift set: the tote plus a printable refill pack you print tonight.

### Profit per sale

Suggested price $28.00 (range $24.00–$34.00, estimate). At an estimated $13 base plus $6 US shipping, $28 with free shipping leaves about $6 after Etsy fees (estimate). Below $24 the margin nearly disappears, and above about $34 it competes with branded gift totes. Margins are thin, so the tote is mainly a gift-set anchor for the Refill Pack.

| Channel | Price | Fees | Product + shipping | You keep | Margin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Etsy | $28.00 | $3.56 | $14.42 | **$14.77** | 53% |

Product cost basis: verified. Cash at risk $34.62; break-even 3 sale(s).

### Asymmetry

- **Risk:** $34.62 and about 6 hours; answer in about 51 days.
- **What you risk:** About 6 hours plus about $20 for one sample; no inventory.
- **What stays open:** Capped: about $6 margin per sale (estimate), a hard Christmas cutoff around Dec 5-10, and a crowded print-on-demand tote market. The upside is mostly strategic: a physical anchor for the Refill Pack gift set and an illustration that is reused for stickers, prints and covers.
- **Bad bet if:** You treat it as a standalone money-maker: it is not very asymmetric on its own (thin margin, deadline, crowded). Skip it if the sample prints poorly on canvas, the chosen phrase has trademark conflicts, or you cannot list by Oct 15.

**Ways to extend:** Sticker sheet of the six items (printable or Redbubble), which is also the pivot; Printable art print of the contents map (8x10 and A4) as a digital listing; Matching zip pouch or bookmark design from the same illustration; Seasonal colorways (tomato ink for the holidays, moss for spring); Use the illustration as the Refill Pack cover so the product family looks coordinated

**Risks:** Thin margins: provider price or shipping changes can erase the roughly $6 profit; recheck costs in the catalog before publishing.; Trademark: the phrase (including 'Gone analog') is unchecked; search before use.; Brand resemblance: AI cameras often look like real models; keep it generic or redraw it.; Print quality: thin lines can break up on canvas; follow the line-weight rule and confirm with the sample.; December delays: provider production and carrier delays can miss Christmas; state cutoffs clearly and avoid promising dates.; Policy: Etsy requires print-on-demand production partners to be disclosed in listings; AI-assisted design should be disclosed too (check current policies).

**Validation test:** List from mockups by Oct 15 and order one sample to check print quality. Promote the gift-set pairing with the refill pack.

**Continue if:** Any tote sales by Nov 20. · **Change direction if:** Totes get views but no sales: offer the illustration as a sticker sheet or printable art instead. · **Stop if:** No sales by Dec 5 (practical POD cutoff for Christmas delivery).

## Season's Scurryings Round Raccoon Ornament + Card

**Trend:** Jimothy the raccoon (Pass) · **Format:** other (print on demand) · **Asymmetry:** Roughly even (49/100)

**Buyer:** Seattle-area gift buyers and Jimothy fans shopping for holiday ornaments and cards (only if revisited)

### Super prompt

```text
You are a holiday-stationery designer and gentle humor writer. Produce the complete design text and specs for a small holiday line built around an ORIGINAL cartoon of a very round, fluffy raccoon, for Seattle-area gift buyers. The character must be your own invention: never describe, trace or adapt a real photo or video frame, existing fan art, figurines or licensed merchandise.

BUYER AND JOB
Seattle-area gift buyers and fans of the round raccoon of Ballard who want a local-pride keepsake for holiday 2026. Job: a cheerful ornament and card that say "local, wholesome, this year" without any team, game, city-office or "official" association.

NAMING (write both; the seller picks one after a trademark search)
Version A (name used): headline "Saint Jimothy", line "Season's Scurryings from Ballard".
Version B (no name): headline "Ballard's Roundest Raccoon", line "Season's Scurryings from Ballard".

DELIVERABLES
1. Ceramic ornament, 3 in round, double-sided, print on demand. Front: the round raccoon wearing a knitted scarf and a small gold halo, headline set in an arc above. Back: "Season's Scurryings from Ballard · 2026" with a tiny pine sprig. Give placement as % of the diameter, a 0.15 in safe zone from the edge, and a minimum text size equal to 9 pt at print size. Master art 3000 x 3000 px, transparent PNG.
2. Holiday card, flat 5 x 7 in, printed 2-up on US Letter landscape and 2-up on A4 landscape (127 x 178 mm each), crop marks, 0.125 in bleed per card. Three front designs: halo raccoon in falling snow; raccoon carrying a tiny wrapped gift; raccoon curled under a pine bough. For each: front text and two back-side message options.
3. Six original inside messages, warm and lightly punny (scurry, round, cozy, neighbors, snow). Do not use or paraphrase slogans already printed on this raccoon's merch, and avoid "trash panda" phrasing.
4. A 60-word "about this design" note for the listing stating that it is original art, an unofficial tribute, not affiliated with any person, team, city office, game or licensed product, and printed and shipped by a print-on-demand partner.

DESIGN
Palette "Ballard Winter": Snow #FAF7F2, Cranberry #B23A48, Pine #3E6B48, Raccoon Grey #8A8D91, Mask Charcoal #2B2B2E, Halo Gold #E3B448. Avoid navy, teal and silver combinations or anything that reads as a sports uniform. Fonts: Fraunces (headlines), Nunito (small text). Style: soft flat vector, rounded shapes, thick friendly outline, simple snow dots.

CONTENT RULES
No team names, logos, uniforms, caps or baseball imagery; no video-game characters or game styling; no bobblehead proportions; no city seals; never the words "official" or "licensed". Do not mention the real animal's health, spine or body condition; keep any roundness humor affectionate. No charity or donation claims. No celebrity names. No claim that the item is handmade or made in Seattle.

DIFFERENTIATION
Existing merch is mostly tees, mugs and stickers. This is a holiday keepsake with local framing and clearly original art. It is probably still contested; say nothing that overpromises.

OUTPUT FORMAT
Section 1: ornament spec (front and back layout, text for both naming versions, color callouts). Section 2: card specs and the three designs with all text. Section 3: the six messages. Section 4: the "about this design" note in both naming versions.
Self-check, fixing failures before answering: [ ] all art described is original and photo-free; [ ] no team, game, bobblehead, city-seal or "official" references; [ ] no existing slogans; [ ] both naming versions supplied; [ ] ornament and both card-sheet sizes given; [ ] no health, charity or handmade claims.
```

Soft flat vector, thick friendly Mask Charcoal #2B2B2E outline, simple shapes, centered for a round 3 in ornament, square 1:1, 3000 x 3000 px, transparent background. Fur in Raccoon Grey #8A8D91 with Snow #FAF7F2 highlights. No text. Do not upload or reference any photo. ### Build and export

5. Connect Printify to Etsy and publish. On Etsy, list the print provider as a production partner, set processing times to the provider's, and add a holiday order-by date taken from Printify's published cutoffs (verify).
6. Card printable: in Canva, make an 11 x 8.5 in design and a 297 x 210 mm design, each holding two 5 x 7 in cards with crop marks; export PDF Print and list as a separate $4 digital download.
7. Photograph the sample on a tree and replace the mockups as soon as it arrives.

### Where to list

### Profit per sale

### Asymmetry

## 'I Remembered. November 2026.' Original Polar Bear Sticker

**Trend:** Remember November 2026 (Prepare) · **Format:** sticker (print on demand) · **Asymmetry:** Unfavorable (35/100)

**Buyer:** Meme-literate teens and young adults decorating laptops, water bottles and phone cases

### Super prompt

```text
You are a sticker designer and meme copywriter. Produce the complete, print-ready design spec for a die-cut sticker of an original cartoon polar bear balanced on its front paws, captioned "I remembered. November 2026.", plus one alternate caption version, for print-on-demand sale.

BUYER AND JOB
Meme-literate US teens and young adults who know the "Remember November 2026" meme (a "meme from the future" that points at a month with nothing in it). They decorate laptops, water bottles and phone cases. The sticker's job: a cheap, giftable in-joke that stays funny all through November because it is the post-payoff punchline, not the "coming" framing that expires on Nov 1.

DELIVERABLE SPEC
- Version A caption: "I remembered. November 2026."
- Version B caption: "Remember November 2026 - I still don't get it"
- Also write 10 alternate captions of 6 words or fewer that work after Nov 1, ranked by how quickly they read on a laptop lid.
- Composition: bear doing a handstand on its front paws, centered; caption on two lines, either arched above or in a speech-bubble banner beneath; the whole design inside one closed silhouette for die cutting.
- Bear (original; must look nothing like the viral AI image): flat 2D cartoon, round chunky body, thick rounded outlines, dot eyes, tiny mint scarf; no photorealism, no fur texture.
- Cut border: a solid Snow white offset of 0.125 in (about 38 px at 300 DPI) around the whole silhouette, with no thin spikes or gaps narrower than 0.0625 in.
- Sizes and files: 3 x 3 in (900 x 900 px), 4 x 4 in (1200 x 1200 px), 5.5 x 5.5 in (1650 x 1650 px) at 300 DPI, transparent PNG; plus a 3000 x 3000 px transparent master for single-sticker marketplaces.
- Readability: caption readable at the 3 in size from about 1 m; minimum cap height 0.15 in at 3 in.

STYLE
Palette: Snow #F4F7FA (bear body, border), Arctic Night #0E1A2B (outlines, caption text), Ice Blue #A9D6E5 (shading), Signal Yellow #FFD23F (banner fill), Scarf Mint #7ED3B2. Font: Fredoka Bold for the caption (free for commercial use), with Nunito ExtraBold as a fallback. Give exact sizes, line breaks, letter case and stroke weights.

CONTENT REQUIREMENTS
- Original wording; deadpan tone; no profanity; nothing political (Nov 3, 2026 is a US election day) and no references to real people or events. Reading level: grade 3-5.
- Accuracy: spell "November 2026" identically in both versions; confirm all files are square, 300 DPI and transparent, and the border is continuous.

DIFFERENTIATION
Competitors copy the AI bear onto apparel with "is coming" text. This uses original art and the after-the-fact punchline, and it matches the "Do You Understand Yet?" wallpaper bear so the two can be bundled.

CONSTRAINTS
- Do not describe, trace or recreate the viral AI polar-bear image or video frames.
- No musician names, song titles, lyrics or melodies; never use the phrase "life goes on".
- No trademarks or brand names.

OUTPUT FORMAT
1. Layer-by-layer spec for Version A and Version B (bear, banner, text, border) with sizes and hex colors.
2. Export list (file names and pixel sizes).
3. The 10 ranked alternates.
4. Canva build steps (7 or fewer), including how to add the white offset border.
5. A ticked self-check: both captions exact; bear is an original flat cartoon; continuous 0.125 in border; files square, 300 DPI, transparent; nothing political; no song, musician or brand.
```

**Image prompt 1 (Sticker bear art: handstand on front paws; ChatGPT images or Midjourney (reuse the wallpaper pack's handstand pose if you a):**

```text
Original cartoon mascot: a round, chunky polar bear drawn as flat 2D vector art with thick rounded Arctic Night #0E1A2B outlines, body Snow #F4F7FA, tiny dot eyes, soft Ice Blue #A9D6E5 shading, a small Scarf Mint #7ED3B2 scarf. Pose: a wobbly handstand on its front paws, back legs in the air, deadpan expression. Full body, centered, wide padding so a caption fits above or below. Square 1:1. Transparent background, or solid pure white #FFFFFF if transparency is not available. Simple sticker style; not photorealistic, no fur texture. No text, no letters. No logos, no real people, no trademarked characters, no watermarks.
```

**Image prompt 2 (Laptop mockup background for listing photos (sticker placed in Canva); Midjourney or ChatGPT images):**

```text
Closed plain matte grey laptop lid on a light wooden desk, top-down view, a reusable water bottle and a phone beside it, soft daylight, photographic. Empty area in the center-right of the lid for placing a sticker later. Vertical 4:5, solid scene background. No device logos (lid completely plain), no existing stickers, no text, no hands. No logos, no real people, no trademarked characters, no watermarks.
```

### Build and export

1. Run the super prompt; generate (or reuse) the handstand bear and check it reads as an original flat cartoon, not the viral image.
2. In Canva create a 3000 x 3000 px design; place the bear, the yellow banner and the caption in Fredoka Bold for Version A; duplicate for Version B.
3. Group everything, then add the white cut border: export the group as a transparent PNG, re-upload it, and apply Edit > Shadows > Outline in Snow white at a size equal to about 0.125 in (Canva Pro needed for transparent PNG export). Check that the outline is continuous with no thin gaps.
4. Export transparent PNGs at 3000 x 3000 px (master) plus 900, 1200 and 1650 px squares.
5. In Printify (connected to Etsy) create kiss-cut sticker products in 3 x 3 in and 4 x 4 in for both versions; publish to Etsy and confirm the print provider is listed as a production partner; state clear production and delivery times and an 'order by' date for delivery in November.
6. Upload both versions to Redbubble as stickers from the 3000 px master (by Oct 15, per the input's test).
7. Place the sticker on the laptop mockup for listing photos; order one sample only if it can arrive before Oct 25.

### Listing copy prompt

```text
You are an Etsy SEO copywriter for meme stickers. Write the Etsy listing for my print-on-demand kiss-cut sticker "I remembered. November 2026." (I will reuse it for Version B: "Remember November 2026 - I still don't get it").
Product facts (use only these; mark anything missing as [CHECK]): original cartoon polar bear doing a handstand, deadpan caption, white border; sizes 3 x 3 in and 4 x 4 in; [PASTE PROVIDER SPECS: material, finish, indoor/outdoor use]; made to order by my print partner; production [PROCESSING DAYS]; US delivery [SHIPPING DAYS]; order-by date for November delivery [ORDER BY DATE]. The art is original and not the viral image.
Buyer: US teens and young adults who follow the "Remember November 2026" meme and decorate laptops and water bottles.

Write:
1. Title under 140 characters starting with the buyer's search phrase "Remember November 2026", then sticker, polar bear, meme, laptop sticker. No ALL CAPS.
2. Exactly 13 Etsy tags, each 20 characters or fewer including spaces, character count after each.
3. Description: two deadpan opening lines; "What you get" bullets; sizes; material and care (from specs only); production and shipping with the order-by date in bold; a 4-question FAQ (Will it arrive in November? Is this the viral bear? - no, original art; Is it waterproof? - from specs only or [CHECK]; Is there a wallpaper to match?).
4. One line stating the illustration was created with the help of AI tools and finished by [YOUR SHOP NAME].
Rules: never mention any musician, song title, lyrics or the audio used in the viral bear video, and never use the phrase 'life goes on'; never describe the product as the viral or AI bear; no trademarked terms or brand names (no laptop, bottle or phone brands) in the title, tags or description; nothing political; do not invent reviews, sales or claims about how popular the meme is.
```

### Where to list

- **Etsy** (primary): Etsy shows searches for 'Remember November 2026' products and Printify handles printing and shipping.
- **Redbubble** (secondary): Zero-admin sticker marketplace already carrying some 2026 polar-bear designs; upload once and let it sit.
- **TikTok** (traffic): Show the sticker inside the wallpaper pack's meme videos rather than making separate posts.

**Positioning:** The punchline after the countdown: an original polar bear sticker for people who were warned about November 2026 and showed up anyway.

### Launch plan

- **Day 0 (Mon Oct 12):** Run the super prompt; generate or reuse the handstand bear; build Versions A and B.
- **Day 1 (Tue Oct 13):** Create Printify kiss-cut sticker products for both versions and publish to Etsy with a clear order-by date; upload both to Redbubble.
- **Day 2 (Wed Oct 14):** Order one sample only if it can arrive before Oct 25; otherwise rely on mockups.
- **Day 3-12 (Oct 15-24):** Add the sticker to the wallpaper pack's teaser videos (sticker on a laptop lid in the final shot).
- **Day 13 (Sun Oct 25):** Main push with the meme videos (Oct 25-Nov 3); mention 'order by [date] to get it in November'.
- **Day 20 (Sun Nov 1):** Nov 1 post: 'I remembered.' with the sticker as the payoff.
- **Day 24 (Thu Nov 5):** Go/pivot check (Nov 5): continue if either version has 5+ sales; if favorites but no sales, give the sticker art away as a free digital bonus with the wallpaper pack.
- **Day 31 (Thu Nov 12):** Stop check (Nov 12): no sales means deactivate the Etsy listing, because print-on-demand delivery would now land after the joke expires.
- **Day 49 (Mon Nov 30):** Nov 30: deactivate everything; remove or leave Redbubble listings as you prefer.

### Marketing channels

| Channel | What to do | How often |
|---|---|---|
| TikTok / Instagram Reels | Sticker as the closing shot of the wallpaper pack's meme videos; 'order by' date in the caption. | In every main-push video, Oct 25-Nov 3. |
| Etsy | Phrase-led title and tags; one listing per version to compare favorites and sales. | Check stats every 2-3 days until Nov 12. |
| Redbubble | Upload once with phrase-led tags. | One-time setup. |

**Search phrases to test (suggestions, not measured volumes):** remember november, polar bear sticker, november 2026, meme sticker, laptop sticker, funny sticker, i remembered, polar bear meme, water bottle sticker, 2026 sticker, die cut sticker

**Hooks:**
- It's November 2026. I remembered. Did you?
- A sticker for people who were warned.
- POV: you've been waiting since 2023 and it's just... November.

### Profit per sale

Suggested price $4.50 (range $3.50–$6.00, estimate: at $4.50 plus shipping charged at roughly cost, margin is roughly $1.50-2 per sticker after an estimated $2 ba). Meme stickers sell as cheap impulse buys; $4.50 plus buyer-paid shipping keeps it near what the competitors' stickers likely charge (estimate; check comparables). Free shipping is not viable at this price because print-on-demand shipping alone costs about as much as the sticker.

| Channel | Price | Fees | Product + shipping | You keep | Margin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Etsy | $4.50 | $1.26 | $6.24 | **$1.00** | 22% |

Product cost basis: mixed. Cash at risk $6.44; break-even 7 sale(s).

### Asymmetry

- **Risk:** $6.44 and about 3 hours; answer in about 49 days.
- **What you risk:** About 3 hours, plus an optional sample of roughly $6 (estimate). No inventory.
- **What stays open:** Small and capped: roughly $1.50-2 margin per Etsy sticker and cents on Redbubble (estimates), with sales possible only until about mid-November because of delivery times.
- **Bad bet if:** You make it as a standalone bet. Thin margins, a hard deadline and print-on-demand shipping lag mean it is not asymmetric on its own; it is worth 3 hours only because it reuses the wallpaper bear and rides the same videos. No sales by Nov 5 is the signal to stop.

**Ways to extend:** Free digital version of the sticker as a bonus with the wallpaper pack.; Wallpaper-plus-sticker bundle photo on both listings.; Reuse the bear for a future 'meme from the future' product only if a new year's version catches on (a different product, judged on its own).

**Risks:** Print-on-demand delivery can push orders past Nov 30, leading to complaints and refunds; show an order-by date.; Takedowns if the bear resembles the viral image or listings reference the song or musician.; Thin margin: shipping costs about as much as the sticker.; Competitors with apparel already rank for the phrase.; Political sensitivity around the Nov 3 election; keep the caption apolitical.

**Validation test:** List 2 caption variants on Redbubble and as Etsy POD stickers by Oct 15, then compare favorites and sales.

**Continue if:** Any variant sells 5+ by Nov 5 · **Change direction if:** Favorites but no sales: bundle as a free bonus with the wallpaper pack · **Stop if:** No sales by Nov 12 (POD delivery would then land after the joke expires)
